Since there are no adders and droppers for the same causal dependency, the order of modifiers does not matter and thus there are no two different states sharing the same configuration, i.e. each configuration represents a state. Thus it is enough for SSDC to consider transition equivalence with respect to configurations, i.e.
\simeq_{\operatorname{t}}
.

Lemma 45
.

Let
\varrho=(E,\to_{\emptyset},{\vartriangleleft},{\blacktriangleright})
be a SSDC. Then for the current causality relation
\to_{X}
of any
state
(X,\to_{X})\in\operatorname{S}\!\left(\varrho\right)
it holds

\to_{X}=(\to_{\emptyset}\setminus\hbox to7.79pt{\vbox to8.94pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.59207pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}

.

To compare DCESs with EBESs, we define a another sub-class of DCESs.

{defi}

Let EBDC denotes a subclass of SSDC with the additional requirements:

(1)

\forall c,a,t\in E\,.\;a\!\blacktriangleright\![c\rightarrow t]\Longrightarrow c=t

(2)

\forall c,d,t\in E\,.\;d\!\vartriangleleft\![c\rightarrow t]\Longrightarrow c\neq t

(3)

\forall c,d_{1},\ldots,d_{n},t\in E\,.\;d_{1}\!\vartriangleleft\![c\rightarrow t]\wedge\dots\wedge d_{n}\!\vartriangleleft\![c\rightarrow t]\Longrightarrow\\
\forall a,b\in\{c,d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\}\,.\;\left(a\neq b\Longrightarrow a\!\blacktriangleright\![b\rightarrow b]\in{\blacktriangleright}\wedge b\!\blacktriangleright\![a\rightarrow a]\in{\blacktriangleright}\right)

The first condition translates disabling into
{\blacktriangleright}
and the second ensures that disabled events cannot be enabled again. The third condition reflects causal unambiguity by
{\vartriangleleft}
such that either the initial cause or one of its droppers can happen.

We adapt the notion of precedence.

{defi}

Let
\vartheta
be a EBDC and
X\in\operatorname{C}\!\left(\vartheta\right)
we define the precedence relation
<_{X}\subseteq X\times X
as
e<_{X}e^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow(e\rightarrow e^{\prime})\lor(e^{\prime}\!\blacktriangleright\![e\rightarrow e])\lor(\exists c\in E\,.\;e\!\vartriangleleft\![c\rightarrow e^{\prime}])
. Let
\leq_{X}
be the reflexive and transitive closure of
<_{X}
.

The relation
<_{X}
indeed represents a precedence relation, and its reflexive transitive closure is a partial order.

Lemma 48
.

Let
\vartheta=(E,\to,{\vartriangleleft},{\blacktriangleright})
be a EBDC,
X\in\operatorname{C}\!\left(\vartheta\right)
, and let
e,e^{\prime}\in X\,.\;e<_{X}e^{\prime}
. Let also
(X_{0},\to_{X_{0}})\!\mapsto_{\operatorname{d}}\!\ldots\!\mapsto_{\operatorname{d}}\!(X_{n},\to_{X_{n}})
with
X_{0}=\emptyset
and
X_{n}=X
be the transition sequence of
X
then
\exists X_{i}\in\{X_{0},\ldots,X_{n}\}\,.\;e\in X_{i}\land e^{\prime}\notin X_{i}
.

Proof D.4
.

Let
(X_{f},\to_{X_{f}})
be the first occurrence of
e
in the sequence
(X_{0},\to_{X_{0}})\!\mapsto_{\operatorname{d}}\!\ldots\!\mapsto_{\operatorname{d}}\!(X_{n},\to_{X_{n}})
, so according to Condition
1
of Definition
6
it is enough to prove that
e^{\prime}\notin X_{f}
.

First, assume that
e\rightarrow e^{\prime}
, then
(e,e^{\prime})\in\to_{\emptyset}
. Then, by Definition
6
, to obtain
(e,e^{\prime})\notin\to_{X_{f-1}}
we need a dropper
d\in X_{f-1}
for
e\rightarrow e^{\prime}
(according to Condition
3
).
But that is impossible, since
e
and
d
will be mutually disabling each other, because of Condition
3
of Definition
D
.
So
(e,e^{\prime})\in\to_{X_{f-1}}
and thus
e^{\prime}\notin X_{f}
because of Condition
2
of Definition
6
.

Second, assume that
e^{\prime}\!\blacktriangleright\![e\rightarrow e]
.
If
e^{\prime}\in X_{f}
then, by Condition
5
of Definition
6
, it follows
e^{\prime}\in X_{f-1}
.
Let
f^{\prime}
be minimal with
e^{\prime}\in X_{f^{\prime}}
by Condition
3
we have
(e,e)\in\to_{X_{f^{\prime}}}
, and—since
\vartheta
is a EBDC and therefore a SSDC—there is no dropper for
e\rightarrow e
and we have
(e,e)\in\to_{X_{f-1}}
. Thus, by Condition
2
,
e\notin X_{f}
contradicting our definition, and therefore the assumption
e^{\prime}\in X_{f}
must be wrong.

Third, assume
\exists c\in E\,.\;e\!\vartriangleleft\![c\rightarrow e^{\prime}]
. Then since EBDC are a subclass of SSDC we have
\nexists a\in E\,.\;a\!\blacktriangleright\![c\rightarrow e^{\prime}]
according to Definition
D
. Then
c\rightarrow e^{\prime}
by Condition
1
of Definition
6
that means
(c,e^{\prime})\in\to_{\emptyset}
. Let us assume that
e^{\prime}\in X_{f}
then either
c
or another dropper
d
with
d\!\vartriangleleft\![c\rightarrow e^{\prime}]
occurred before
e^{\prime}
that is impossible because of the mutual disabling in Condition
3
of Definition
D
. So
e^{\prime}\notin X_{f}
.

Lemma 49
.

\leq_{X}
is a partial order over
X
.

Proof D.5
.

Let
e,e^{\prime}\in X\,.\;e<_{X}e^{\prime}
and let
(\emptyset=X_{0},\to_{X_{0}})\ldots(X_{n}=X,\to_{X_{n}})
be the transition sequence of
X
.
Let
X_{h}
(
X_{j}
) be the configurations where
e
(respectively
e^{\prime}
) occurs first.
By Lemma
48
then
h<j
.
Since
\leq_{X}
is the reflexive and transitive closure of
<_{X}
we have
e\leq_{X}e^{\prime}\Longrightarrow h\leq j
.
For anti-symmetry, assume that
e^{\prime}\leq_{X}e
. By Lemma
48
, we have
j\leq h
, but
h\leq j
. Hence
h=j
.
The equality
h=j
implies that
e=e^{\prime}
because otherwise
h<j
and
j<h
that is a contradiction.

Let
\operatorname{P}\!\left(\vartheta\right)=\{(X,\leq_{X})\mid X\in\operatorname{C}\!\left(\vartheta\right)\}
denote the set of posets of the EBDC
\vartheta
. We show that the transitions of a EBDC
\vartheta
can be extracted from its posets.

Theorem 50
.

Let
\vartheta=(E,\to_{\emptyset},{\vartriangleleft},{\blacktriangleright})
be a EBDC and
(X,\to_{X}),(Y,\to_{Y})\in\operatorname{S}\!\left(\vartheta\right)
with
X\subset Y
. Then

\left(\forall e,e^{\prime}\in Y\,.\;e\neq e^{\prime}\land e^{\prime}\leq_{Y}e\implies e^{\prime}\in X\right)\Longleftrightarrow(X,\to_{X})\!\mapsto_{\operatorname{d}}\!(Y,\to_{Y}).

Proof D.6
.

holds by assumption.

.
Consider now
e\!\blacktriangleright\![e^{\prime}\rightarrow e^{\prime}]
. If
e,e^{\prime}\in Y
, we have
e^{\prime}\leq_{Y}e
. Therefore by assumption
e^{\prime}\in X
that fulfills Condition
5
.

Let us now assume
(X,\to_{X})\!\mapsto_{\operatorname{d}}\!(Y,\to_{Y})
, and
e,e^{\prime}\in Y
with
e\neq e^{\prime}
and
e^{\prime}\leq_{Y}e
by Lemma
48
it follows
e^{\prime}\in X
.

The following defines an encoding of an EBES into an EBDC. Furthermore the encoding preserves posets. Figure
12
provides an example where conflicts with impossible events are dropped for simplicity.

{defi}

Let
\xi=\left(E,{\leadsto},{\rightarrowtail},l\right)
be an EBES,
\left\{X_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
an enumeration of its bundles, and
\left\{x_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
a set of fresh events, i.e.
\left\{x_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}\cap E=\emptyset
.

Then
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)=(E^{\prime},\to_{\emptyset},{\vartriangleleft},{\blacktriangleright})
such that:

(1)

E^{\prime}=E\cup\left\{x_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

(2)

\to_{\emptyset}=\left\{x_{i}\!\to\!e\mid X_{i}\!\rightarrowtail\!e\right\}\cup\left\{x_{i}\!\to\!x_{i}\mid i\in I\right\}

(3)

{\vartriangleleft}\left\{d\!\vartriangleleft\![x_{i}\rightarrow e]\mid d\in X_{i}\land X_{i}\!\rightarrowtail\!e\right\}

(4)

{\blacktriangleright}=\{e^{\prime}\!\blacktriangleright\![e\rightarrow e]\mid e\!\leadsto\!e^{\prime}\}
.

We show that the encoding yields an EBDC.

Lemma 51
.

Let
\xi
be an EBES. Then
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
is an EBDC.

Proof D.7
.

First
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
is a DCES. The Definitions of
\to_{\emptyset},{\vartriangleleft}
, and
{\blacktriangleright}
in Definition
D
ensure the Conditions
6
.
1
and
6
.
3
. According to the definition of
{\vartriangleleft}
in Condition
D
.
3
all dropped causes are the fresh events that cannot be added by
{\blacktriangleright}
(Condition
D
.
4
). So Condition
6
.
5
also holds.

Second,
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
is a SSDC since all dropped events are fresh and because these fresh events are never added by
{\blacktriangleright}
, the conditions of Definition
D
are satisfied.

Third,
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
is a EBDC. The Conditions
D
.
1
and
2
hold by definition. Bundle members in
\xi
mutually disable each other. Then according to the Condition
D
.
4
, the Condition
D
.
3
holds. Therefore
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
is a EBDC.

Before comparing an EBES with its encoding according to posets, we show that they have the same configurations.

Lemma 52
.

Let
\xi=\left(E,{\leadsto},{\rightarrowtail}\right)
be an EBES. Then
\operatorname{C}\!\left(\xi\right)=\operatorname{C}\!\left(\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)\right)
.

Proof D.8
.

First,
\forall X\subseteq E\,.\;X\in\operatorname{C}\!\left(\xi\right)\Longrightarrow X\in\operatorname{C}\!\left(\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)\right)
.

According to §
2.2
,
X\in\operatorname{C}\!\left(\xi\right)
means there is a trace
t=e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}
in
\xi
such that
X=\bar{t}
. Let us prove that
t
corresponds to a transition sequence in
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
leading to
X
. I.e. let us prove that there exists a transition sequence
(X_{0},\to_{X_{0}})\!\mapsto_{\operatorname{d}}\!\ldots\!\mapsto_{\operatorname{d}}\!(X_{n},\to_{X_{n}})
with
X_{0}=\emptyset,\to_{X_{0}}=\to_{\emptyset}
and
X_{n}=X
such that
X_{i}=X_{i-1}\cup\left\{e_{i}\right\}
for
1\leq i\leq n
, and
\to_{X_{i}}
is defined according to Lemma
45
. This means we have to prove that
(X_{i-1},\to_{X_{i-1}})\!\mapsto_{\operatorname{d}}\!(X_{i},\to_{X_{i}})
for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Since
X_{i}=X_{i-1}\cup\left\{e_{i}\right\}
, we have
X_{i-1}\subset X_{i}
and so Condition
1
in Definition
6
holds.
Next, let us prove that
\forall e\in X_{i}\setminus X_{i-1}\,.\;\{e^{\prime}\mid(e^{\prime},e)\in\to_{X_{i-1}}\}\subset X_{i-1}
, i.e.
\{e^{\prime}\mid(e^{\prime},e)\in(\to_{\emptyset}\setminus\hbox to7.79pt{\vbox to8.94pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.59207pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\emptyset,X_{i-1}})\cup\,\hbox to7.79pt{\vbox to8.94pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 11.81 L 10.23 5.91 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\emptyset,X_{i-1}}\}\subseteq X_{i-1}
.
By Lemma
45
and the Definition of
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
,
\to_{\emptyset}
contains only fresh (and impossible) events as causes and the elements
x\in X_{i}
of bundles
{X_{i}}{\rightarrowtail}{e}
are droppers of these fresh events.
But since each of these bundles is satisfied, each of these fresh events in
\to_{\emptyset}
is dropped. Furthermore, there cannot be added causality in
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
for
e
, except disabling of
e
, but this is not possible since it occurs in a configuration. Therefore
\{e^{\prime}\mid(e^{\prime},e)\in(\to_{\emptyset}\setminus\hbox to7.79pt{\vbox to8.94pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.59207pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 11.81 L -10.23 5.91 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\emptyset,X_{i-1}})\cup\,\hbox to7.79pt{\vbox to8.94pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 11.81 L 10.23 5.91 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\emptyset,X_{i-1}}\}\subseteq X_{i-1}
for all
e\in X_{i}
and all
1\leq i\leq n
.
Condition
3
follows from Lemma
46
.
Condition
4
of Definition
6
holds by Definition
D
.
Since in the transition
(X_{i-1},\to_{X_{i-1}})\!\mapsto_{\operatorname{d}}\!(X_{i},\to_{X_{i}})
only one event—namely
e_{i}
—occurs, the last Condition
5
is satisfied.

On the other hand let
X\in\operatorname{C}\!\left(\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)\right)
and
(X_{0},\to_{X_{0}})\!\mapsto_{\operatorname{d}}\!\ldots\!\mapsto_{\operatorname{d}}\!(X_{n},\to_{X_{n}})
such that
X_{0}=\emptyset,\to_{X_{0}}=\to_{\emptyset}
.
By Lemma
47
, we can assume
e_{i+1}:=X_{i+1}\setminus X_{i}
.
We have to show that
t=e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}
is a trace in
\xi
.
By Definition
2.2
, this means
\forall 1\leq i,j\leq n\,.\;e_{i}\!\leadsto\!e_{j}\implies i<j
and
\forall 1\leq i\leq n\,.\;\forall Y\subseteq E\,.\;Y\!\rightarrowtail\!e_{i}\implies\overline{t_{i-1}}\cap Y\neq\emptyset
.
Assume
e_{i}\!\leadsto\!e_{j}
.
By Definition
D
, we have
e_{j}\!\blacktriangleright\![e_{i}\rightarrow e_{i}]
.
By Definition
D
and Lemma
48
, then
e_{i}<_{X}e_{j}
and
\exists i^{\prime}<n\,.\;e_{i}\in X_{i^{\prime}}\wedge e_{j}\notin X_{i^{\prime}}
.
But, since
e_{j}\in X
, the first Condition holds.
Next assume
Y\!\rightarrowtail\!e_{i}
.
By Definition
D
, there is a fresh and impossible cause
x_{i}
for
e_{i}
in the initial causality (
(x_{i},e_{i}),(x_{i},x_{i})\in\to_{\emptyset}
), there is no dropper for
(x_{i},x_{i})
, and the elements of
Y
are exactly the droppers for
(x_{i},e_{i})
(
y\in Y\iff y\!\vartriangleleft\![x_{i}\rightarrow e_{i}]
).
Since
e_{i}\in X_{i}
and by Condition
2
of Definition
6
, it follows
(x_{i},e_{i})\notin\to_{X_{i-1}}
(since
x_{i}
never becomes enabled).
By Condition
3
, there must be a
j<i
such that
e_{j}\!\vartriangleleft\![x_{i}\rightarrow e_{i}]
(since
(x_{i},e_{i})\in\to_{\emptyset}
), thus we have
e_{j}\in\overline{t_{i-1}}\cap Y
and we are done.

Finally we show that the encoding preserves posets.

Lemma 53
.

For each EBES
\xi
there is a DCES, namely
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
, such that
\operatorname{P}\!\left(\xi\right)=\operatorname{P}\!\left(\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)\right)
.

Proof D.9
.

Let
p=(X,\leq_{X})
, then
X\in\operatorname{C}\!\left(\xi\right)
by the definition of posets of EBESs. Then according to Lemma
52
:
X\in\operatorname{C}\!\left(\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)\right)
. On the other hand, let
\leq_{X}^{\prime}
be the partial order defined for
X
in
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
as in Definition
D
. This means that we should prove that
\leq_{X}=\leq_{X}^{\prime}
. Since
\leq_{X},\leq_{X}^{\prime}
are the reflexive and transitive closures of
\prec_{X},<_{X}
respectively, we have to prove that
\prec_{X}=<_{X}
. In other words we have to prove
\forall e,e^{\prime}\in X\,.\;e\prec_{X}e^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow e^{\prime}<_{X}e
.

Let us start with
e\prec_{X}e^{\prime}\Longrightarrow e<_{X}e^{\prime}
. According to §
2.2

e\prec_{X}e^{\prime}
means
\exists Y\subseteq E\,.\;e\in Y\!\rightarrowtail\!e^{\prime}\lor e\!\leadsto\!e^{\prime}
. If
\exists Y\subseteq E\,.\;e\in{\rightarrowtail}{Y}{e}^{\prime}
then
\exists c\in E^{\prime}\,.\;e\!\vartriangleleft\![c\rightarrow e^{\prime}]
, where
E^{\prime}
is the set of events of
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
, by the definition of
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
(Definition
D
). This means
e<_{X}e^{\prime}
according to the definition of
<_{X}
(Definition
D
). If
e\!\leadsto\!e^{\prime}
, then
\neg e^{\prime}\!\leadsto\!e
because otherwise
e
and
e^{\prime}
are in conflict. This means
e^{\prime}\!\blacktriangleright\![e\rightarrow e]
according to Definition
D
that means
e<_{X}e^{\prime}
according to Definition
D
.

Let us consider the other direction:
e<_{X}e^{\prime}\Longrightarrow e\prec_{X}e^{\prime}
.
e<_{X}e^{\prime}
means
\exists c\in E^{\prime}\,.\;e\!\vartriangleleft\![c\rightarrow e^{\prime}]\lor e^{\prime}\!\blacktriangleright\![e\rightarrow e]
, where
E^{\prime}
is the set of events of
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
, according to the definition of
<_{X}
in Definition
D
. The third option, where
e\!\to\!e^{\prime}
, is rejected, because all initial causes in
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
are the fresh impossible events. If
\exists c\in E^{\prime}\,.\;e\!\vartriangleleft\![c\rightarrow e^{\prime}]
then
\exists Y\subseteq E\,.\;e\in Y\!\leadsto\!e^{\prime}
according to the definition of
{\vartriangleleft}
in
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
. This means
e\prec_{X}e^{\prime}
by the definition of
\prec_{X}
in §
2.2
. If on the other hand
e^{\prime}\!\blacktriangleright\![e\rightarrow e]
, then
e\!\leadsto\!e^{\prime}
according to the definition of
\operatorname{dces}\!\left(\xi\right)
that means
e\prec_{X}
in §
2.2
.

So we have
\prec_{X}=<_{X}
that implies
\leq_{X}=\leq^{\prime}_{X}
.
